Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 0.3% on Tuesday 10-Nov, which leaves it up 2.0% in the week, 2.3% over the fortnight and 3.6% over the month; over three months it is down 0.1%, over twelve months up 14.9%. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 3-Nov close, the fortnight the 10 sessions from the 27-Oct close and the month the 21 sessions from the 12-Oct close, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's bases; the industry figures are price moves of each industry's index over the same sessions (the Market chart, 3 and 12 months). Since the 30-Oct month-end the index is up 1.8%.

  • Tuesday 10-Nov: a narrow day. Defence led (+1.2%), then Financials (+0.9%), Professional, business and consumer services (+0.6%), and Technology, Consumer staples and Materials (+0.5% each). Transportation (0.6%), Utilities (0.5%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (0.4%) and Healthcare (0.2%) fell; Industrials and capital goods rose 0.3%, level with the index.
  • The week: Financials +3.4%, Media +3.3%, Consumer discretionary +2.9%, Real assets +2.7% and Consumer staples +2.4% led; Transportation (3.5%), Technology (1.5%) and Energy, commodities and metals mining (0.4%) fell; Defence was flat and Industrials and capital goods +0.1%.
  • The month's leaders (21 sessions to 10-Nov): Healthcare +8.8%, Consumer staples +8.2%, Consumer discretionary +7.9% and Real assets +7.5%. The laggards: Technology (3.9%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (3.8%), Transportation (3.5%) and Industrials and capital goods (0.3%).
  • Over three months: Healthcare +8.5%, Consumer staples +6.7% and Professional, business and consumer services and Infrastructure (+3.8% and +3.9%) lead; Defence (8.8%), Real assets (5.2%), Media (3.7%) and Technology (2.8%) lag.

Tuesday's market rise did not lift the book's weakest holdings: most of them faded intraday. Alfen gapped up and closed at its low, (2.1%), while its median peer rose 0.2%; Arteche gapped up, stalled at €27.7 and closed near its low; Volex rose to 556p and closed near its low on light volume (0.64x); D'Ieteren made an outside day and closed at its low, (2.5%), in a rising market; Avanza reversed at its falling 100D MA on 1.52x volume. Against that, Theon rose 2.0% on its Q3 update against Defence's +1.2%, Xvivo rose while all four of its cohort peers fell, and Helios Towers closed at its highest since 2-Jun. What would confirm that the weak holdings' rallies are being sold: a second gap-up that closes near the low; an up day on 1.5 times the 50D average volume closing in the top half of the range would say the opposite.

The defensive rotation resumed this week after pausing. Consumer staples rose 2.4% in the week against the index's 2.0%, and 0.5% on Tuesday against 0.3%; Healthcare (+8.8%) and Consumer staples (+8.2%) still lead the month. The book's Consumer staples holdings were mixed against it on the week: Hilton Food +3.5% (a new 52W closing high on 0.33x volume) beat it, Carlsberg +2.4% matched it and Fevertree +1.1% lagged it. What would confirm the rotation: Consumer staples leading again into the 13-Nov Friday reading; Financials and Consumer discretionary leading the week say cyclicals are bid alongside it.

The AI and data-centre complex remains the book's weak spot: Technology (3.9%) is the worst industry over the month and Industrials and capital goods (0.3%) the fourth worst. The book's holdings in those two industries fell much further over the month: Arteche (22.9%), Volex (21.1%), Hexatronic (9.0%), Alfen (8.9%). Over the week Hexatronic fell 14.7%, Arteche 8.2% and Volex 4.6%, against Industrials and capital goods' +0.1% and Technology's (1.5%), so most of the week's fall was the stocks' own.

Industrials and capital goods, the book's largest industry (about 23.4% of capital on current values: Sdiptech 7.29%, Volex 5.64%, IMCD 3.70%, Arteche 3.62%, Alfen 3.10%), read Industry RS 26 at the 6-Nov reading, from 46 at 30-Oct. The industry's index is down only 0.3% over the month: its Industry RS fell because other industries rose, not because it collapsed. The reading stands until Fri 13-Nov. If the four sales the rules require at Tue 17-Nov are made, IMCD (3.70%) would be the only holding left in the industry.

Defence fell 8.8% over three months, and Theon's Industry RS is 13. Tuesday's +1.2% followed Monday's +3.9%; the industry is flat on the week and up 6.8% over the month. The Bundestag budget committee (12-Nov) and vote (27-Nov) are its next tests (Theon's Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 5-Nov). The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting table holds Defence at Underweight.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of the ideal portfolio (the table set on 6-Nov-2026, unchanged; the next is due about 13-Nov): Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Defence, Media and Telecoms; Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included; Momentum Underweight and Fragility Double Underweight. Its logic, in brief: risk-off beneath an index near its high on narrow breadth, defensives leading: own the defensives with rising estimates, avoid the leveraged and rate-sensitive. The week's tape was mixed against the table: Overweight Consumer staples (+2.4%) beat the index (+2.0%), but Overweight Energy, commodities and metals mining fell (0.4%) while Underweight Media (+3.3%) and Double Underweight Consumer discretionary (+2.9%) and Real assets (+2.7%) led. The book's Consumer staples weight (Carlsberg 4.61%, Hilton Food 4.81%, Fevertree 2.47%, about 11.9%) is about half its Industrials and capital goods weight today, and would be about three times it after the four sales.

Volex rose to within 0.4% of its rising absolute 200D MA on Tuesday 10-Nov and was turned back on light volume: it closed at 540p, +0.7%, near the day's low, still 3.2% below that MA, two sessions before its interim results.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Volex against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+2.4% over 20 sessions, +0.3% over 5, slowing). The 150D above the absolute 200D: passes. The 50D above the 150D and absolute 200D: passes, but the 50D has fallen 1.3% in 5 sessions.

  • Stage 3 flags present: the largest one-day fall since the advance began, after good news (19-Oct, (11.6%)); wider swings ((25.6%) against 6% to 7% pullbacks in August and September); lower lows (520p on 6-Nov below 22-Oct's 532p); more down days than up days (14 against 11 since 6-Oct); the SP tracking the rising absolute 200D MA from 30-Oct to 3-Nov, breaking it, and on 10-Nov turned back from it. Four of six trend-health tests pass, two fail; five of six Stage 3 flags show. Distribution days in 25 sessions (dashboard): 5 (8, 15, 19 and 20-Oct, and 5-Nov), up from 2 at 13-Oct and 4 at 27-Oct and 3-Nov. 8-Oct leaves the window on Thursday 12-Nov, results day, so a results-day distribution day keeps the count at 5 and a results day without one takes it to 4.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "L20D OBV is very negative at c. 1.5x the up days". It is worse than that. Over 20 sessions the up/down volume ratio is 0.54 (dashboard basis), so down-day volume runs about 1.9 times up-day volume; over 5 sessions the dashboard reads 0.31, about 3.2 times. A healthy pullback dries up into its low; this one has not.

Volume at the MAs: every break came on more volume than the reclaim before it. 3. RS

Volex's Sector RS rose 7 points on Tuesday, to above the 50 line; Stock RS slipped a point and sits on the 50 line; Industry RS, at 26, has not moved. Readings of 10-Nov: Stock RS 50, Sector RS 57, Industry RS 26 (9-Nov: 51, 50, 26; 6-Nov: 46, 45, 26).

  • Volex's Stock RS: 97 on 9-Oct, 65 on 23-Oct, 85 on 29-Oct, 78 at the 30-Oct reading, 46 on 6-Nov, 50 on 10-Nov: 47 points down in a month, below the 70 line and on the 50 line (a reading of 49 or lower meets "below 50").
  • Volex's Sector RS (Engineered components, polymers and electronics manufacturing): 74 on 9-Oct, 70 at 30-Oct, 45 on 6-Nov, 57 on 10-Nov: 12 points back in two sessions while Volex's Stock RS gained 4. The sector is recovering without Volex.
  • Volex's Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 on 9-Oct, 46 at 30-Oct, 26 on 4, 6, 9 and 10-Nov. The Friday 6-Nov reading of 26 is the sell signal and stands until the Friday 13-Nov reading. Industry RS moves in steps of about 6.7 points (one place among 16 industries), so a reading of 30 or more needs one place.

The order of the turn: the industry first, then Volex, then its sector: a rotation carrying the stock, which Volex has since outrun on the way down. Volex's Industry RS fell first (79 to 66 by 16-Oct, while Volex's Stock RS was still 94); Volex's Stock RS fell 29 points in its update week (65 on 23-Oct); the sector held 69 to 79 until 30-Oct and fell 25 points in the next week. Over 20 sessions Healthcare rose 9.2% and Consumer staples 7.3% against the Stoxx Europe 600's +3.7%, Industrials' (0.6%) and Technology's (3.7%) (market file): no turn yet.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Prior industry/sector RS fall was not mirrored by Volex RS, unlike this time". Confirmed: when Volex's Industry RS last stood near the line, at 40 in March 2026, Volex's Stock RS held 84 to 86 and Volex rose 63% from its 17-Mar low; this time Volex's Stock RS fell further and faster than its industry's. Test: Volex's Stock RS after the 12-Nov results. Back above 70 would say the stock leads again; a Stock RS of 49 or lower at the 30-Nov month-end completes SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)).

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus has not moved since 6-Nov while the SP fell 22.7% from its 6-Oct high; the estimates still rise at every horizon over 1, 3 and 6 months, at a one-month pace slowed to 2% to 3%.

  • Volex's FY2027 (year to March 2027) EPS, $0.521 (10-Nov): +2.9% over 1 month, +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6 months. FY2028 EPS, $0.579: +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%. FY2029 EPS, $0.638: +2.2%, +13.1% over 3 months (first estimate 30-Apr).
  • Volex's FY2027 revenue, $1,411m: +1.8%, +6.8%, +12.2%; FY2027 EBITDA, $192.6m: +2.0%, +10.5%, +21.4%; FY2028 and FY2029 at about the same pace (7 analysts each).
  • Trajectory: the later years rise a little more slowly than FY2027 over 3 months, so implied FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth eased from 11.7% (31-Jul) to 11.1%, and FY2028 to FY2029 from 11.6% to 10.3%: analysts are raising the level faster than the trajectory, a mild pulling forward.
  • Margin: Volex's implied FY2027 EBITDA margin is 13.7% (13.2% at 31-Jul), FY2028's 13.8% and FY2029's 13.9%: the upgrades are margin first.

On Monday 9-Nov, Volex's EPS, EBITDA and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth fell from +100% (7 of 7 raising, 6-Nov) to +71% (5 raising, none cutting), and held at +71% on 10-Nov, with no change in any consensus figure. No analyst is counted as cutting. Hypothesis: two raises from late July or early August have left the counting window (100 days); the cause is not stated in the data file. It is not a cut, but it ends the run of unanimous raising that has held since 30-Sep.

The SP led the estimates up to 6-Oct and has fallen since while FY2027 EPS rose about 3%: either the SP is leading the estimates down, or the market has de-rated estimates it still believes. On 3-Nov you wrote: "Almost always, price moves first". On 10-Nov you wrote: "The "bear market" is positive fundamentals yet falling SP; it is loud and clear and true here". Hypothesis 1, price leads: the market is discounting a FY2028 data-centre risk that consensus does not show: the largest customer (about 18.7% of FY2026 revenue) in the 2026 to 2028 re-award window (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov), now joined by a US policy risk to new data-centre approvals (Sector Digest note, 10-Nov). Hypothesis 2, de-rating: the chain is upgraded and de-rated at once (item 9 (Peers)). The tests are the memo's, all on Thursday 12-Nov: H1 underlying operating profit at or above $74m; working capital falling from 28.4% of revenue; the data-centre share of Complex Industrial Technology restated.

On 10-Nov you wrote: "Recently upgraded guidance in some ways but also downgraded it in others". That matches the 19-Oct update as the Investment Analyst read it (Sector Digest insight, 3-Nov): FY2027 profit guided "towards the upper end" of expectations, but data-centre revenue below the second half of FY2026, about $10m pulled into the first half by customer stock-building, and the second half guided broadly flat on the first.

4. Consensus by named FY

Sdiptech's consensus has not changed since 5-Nov; over the month FY2026 EPS is weaker while FY2027 and FY2028 are raised, and the implied margin slips.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS (8.0%) in a month to SEK 13.16, reversing September's 8.1% rise, (15.5%) over 12 months; revenue +2.9% to SEK 5,210m and EBITDA +3.1% to SEK 1,214m, so the EPS cut arises below the EBITDA line
  • FY2027: EPS +2.1% in a month to SEK 17.79 (+7.9% over 3 months); revenue +5.2% to SEK 5,647m; EBITDA +2.8% to SEK 1,416m
  • FY2028: EPS +3.7% in a month to SEK 19.97 (+9.2% over 3 months); revenue +5.9% to SEK 6,047m; EBITDA +3.4% to SEK 1,523m

The later year rises faster, so analysts are raising Sdiptech's trajectory, not pulling earnings forward: implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 rose from 10.6% (30-Sep) to 12.3%. The margin keeps slipping: the implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA divided by revenue) is 25.1%, from 25.7% at 30-Sep and 25.9% at 30-Apr. The third-quarter report explains part of it: Supply Chain & Transportation's margin fell to 18.5% on raw material costs at Mecno, which DNB Carnegie expects to weigh through the fourth quarter; operating cash flow fell 44% to SEK 143m, a negative the consensus lines do not show (earnings review, 23-Oct).

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "[+] SS Breadth and upgrades broadly positive, apart from 2026 EPS". Confirmed. The FY2026 EPS cut has named parts worth about 3 of its 8 points: the SEK 13m early bond-redemption charge (about 2%) and DNB Carnegie's 1% trim on higher segment costs (earnings review). Hypothesis: the rest is brokers' different EPS definitions; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (31-Oct) calls FactSet's EPS consensus "a median of two brokers on two different EPS definitions".

The estimates and the SP have parted since mid-October: FY2027 EPS rose 2.1% from 30-Sep to 10-Nov while the SP closed 4.4% below its 14-Oct high. Your 3-Nov note says "Almost always, price moves first", so the 30-Nov estimates will test whether the SP is leading a turn in the estimates. Sdiptech still passes your 3-Nov rule ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule").

Against it: 9-Nov's 58,899 shares were the heaviest of the decline. If your reading is right, 9-Nov drops out of the evidence both ways: it is neither a selling climax nor a true distribution day, and what remains is the 2 to 6-Nov run of Arteche's own down days (four of five sessions, 2-Nov on 1.41x and 5-Nov on 1.61x the 50D average) and a failed 10-Nov rally. The selling-climax hypothesis is now unconfirmed and weaker; a close below €24.4 (the 9-Nov low) would refute it.

On 10-Nov you wrote: "The price is now quite a bit below the prior clear floor level in Aug-26 of c. 29 EUR". The data agree: the August floor was a band of closes from €28.0 (1-Sep) to €28.8 (5 to 19-Aug); Arteche first closed below it on 5-Nov (€27.7). A broken floor tends to become resistance: the 3 and 4-Nov rallies stopped at €29.8 and €30.0 intraday, 10-Nov's at €27.7.

The MAs: Arteche is below all seven, and only the absolute 200D MA still rises. It is 11.6% below its 20D MA (€30.42), 16.4% below the 50D (€32.18), 18.7% below the 150D (€33.09) and 13.4% below the absolute 200D MA (€31.06); the 50D has turned down ((0.43%) over five sessions) and the 150D has gone flat (€33.10 to €33.09 since 5-Nov). Arteche lost every MA on light volume (0.60x to 0.87x, 8 to 30-Oct) and moved away from them on heavy volume (1.41x on 2-Nov, 1.61x on 5-Nov, 2.29x on 9-Nov). Precedent: at the 29-Jul and 1-Sep lows Arteche held within 1.9% of its rising absolute 200D MA; this time it has closed below for eight sessions, more than 5% below since 2-Nov.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: ratios leave out the one mechanical-volume day in Arteche's 50D average (the 18-Sep index review, 380,116 shares: about 25,500 shares a day without it, 32,600 with), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets. 18-Sep leaves the 50D window about 27-Nov.

On 10-Nov you wrote: "Q - How can 09-Nov display as green in SP chart but be a red distribution day in the volume chart?" Both charts are right: they colour two different comparisons.

  • The SP chart colours each candle by the close against the same day's open. 9-Nov opened at €26.7 and closed at €26.9, above the open, so its candle is green.
  • The volume chart colours each bar by the close against the previous day's close. 9-Nov closed at €26.9 against €27.1 on 6-Nov, (0.7%), a down day, so its bar is red; it is solid red, a distribution day, because its 58,899 shares were 1.79 times the 50D average on the dashboard's basis, above your 1.25x line.
  • Read together, they describe the day: sellers took Arteche from €27.1 to €24.4, and buyers took it back to €26.9 inside the session, but not to the previous close.
  • 10-Nov is the mirror image: a red candle (opened €27.4, closed €26.9) above a green volume bar, because the dashboard counts an unchanged close as an up day. A consequence: the dashboard's five-session up/down volume ratio (0.70) counts 10-Nov's 30,109 shares as up-day volume on a day the SP did not rise.
  • One more display point: the chart also draws 5-Nov solid red (1.26 times that day's 50D average), while the dashboard's count of distribution days, set against the latest 50D average, reads 1.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) in the last 25 sessions: two, both down days, both this month.

  • 5-Nov: 40,032 shares, €1.11m, 1.61x (1.26x); (4.5%) after a 1.4% gap up, closing at 0.25 of the range; a sector selling day (Siemens Energy (4.7%) on 2.8x, ABB (4.2%) on 2.6x, Pfisterer (4.8%) on 2.1x).
  • 9-Nov: 58,899 shares, €1.58m, 2.29x (1.79x); (0.7%) after a 1.5% gap down, a low of €24.4 and a close at 0.96 of the range; a sector buying day Arteche missed (R&S Group Holding +7.0% on 2.4x, Legrand +3.3% on 1.5x), shared only by Cembre ((0.7%) on 1.5x).
  • 10-Nov, below the line: 30,109 shares, €0.81m, 1.18x (0.92x); unchanged after a 1.9% gap up, closing at 0.11 of the range.

Distribution days on your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average or more, last 25 sessions): three, all since 2-Nov (2-Nov 1.41x, 5-Nov 1.61x, 9-Nov 2.29x); the dashboard counts one (9-Nov). A month ago there were two (28-Sep at 1.32x and 1-Oct at 1.95x). No accumulation day has come in 25 sessions on either basis; 10-Nov, the week's only up day on the dashboard's rule, came on 1.18x (0.92x).

Buyers are thinner in this decline than in the last one of similar size. Up-day over down-day volume (average shares) is 0.70 over five sessions (dashboard), 0.77 over 20 (dashboard and volume script agree) and 0.75 over the whole decline from 30-Sep (11 up or unchanged days averaging 19,700 shares, 18 down days averaging 26,200). In the May to July 2026 decline, without the 25-Jun placement, the ratio was about 1.0.

Volume has risen as the price fell: Arteche traded an average of 14,900 shares a day in the week to 27-Oct, 19,200 in the week to 3-Nov and 35,800 in the week to 10-Nov. Rising volume on falling prices is the pattern of distribution, not of a decline drying up; the dashboard's 20D-against-50D volume trend (0.84) still reads light because it averages in the quiet October weeks.

Precedent: the June to July decline ended with a 6.1% up day on 2.14x on 30-Jul, closing at its high; the March 2026 decline ended quietly at €24.35. 9-Nov had the heavy volume and the close at the high but not the up close, and 10-Nov supplied neither: no low is in yet on this evidence, though 10-Nov's above-average volume came without a lower low.

Arteche's relative price is 43.8% below its 52W high (12-May-2026), its lowest since 6-Mar-2026, and 18.7% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed below on 22-Oct. It was 18.8% below its 52W high at the 30-Sep month-end and 34.7% below at the 30-Oct reading. Relative price confirmed the SP's new closing low of 9-Nov and fell further on 10-Nov, as it confirmed the SP's 8-May high.

Confirms a turn: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 38,300 shares, €1.03m at €26.9), closing in the top third of its range above €27.7, then a close above €29.0. Confirms the decline continues: a close below €24.4 on more than 25,500 shares.

On the figures: volume against the 50D average on the volume script's basis, without the mechanical-volume day of 18-Sep; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets.

It was not a distribution day (that needs 1.25x or more on a down day). Hypothesis: holders sell every uptick, but no large seller is forcing the price down.

The only heavy-volume days in 25 sessions are the results days, and the sellers won both: 4-Nov, 441,849 shares (€4.66m), 2.67x (2.51x), +1.2% but closing at 0.36 of the range after a 1.9% gap-down open; 5-Nov, 286,304 shares (€2.89m), 1.70x (1.60x), (4.4%), closing at 0.25.

Up-day volume trails down-day volume, and the gap widened over the week: the dashboard's ratio is 0.85 over 20 sessions and 0.63 over 5 sessions (0.84 on 9-Nov). Over the decline since 17-Aug the up-day to down-day volume ratio is 0.88, with 6 heavy down days against 2 heavy up days; it was 0.69 and 0.96 over the two earlier declines of similar size (February to March, May to July) and 2.27 over the spring advance. Volume over 20 sessions is 1.07 times the 50D average: participation is not drying up, as it would at a selling climax's end.

Precedent: the sign that ended the March decline has still not appeared. After the 20-Mar low Alfen rose 8.6% on 2.2x on 23-Mar, closing at its high, and never closed below that day's low again. Alfen's Sector RS (Electrical equipment, grid and automation) jumped to 17 on 10-Nov, from 6 on 9-Nov and 4 on 4-Nov (the lowest in the data file, which starts in October 2025); its Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods) is 26.

  • Industry RS: 79 on 9-Oct, 46 at the 30-Oct reading, 26 at the Friday 6-Nov reading and on 10-Nov, below the line of 30 (information only for Alfen). Industry RS moves in steps of about six to seven points, so one Friday reading can take it back across 30.

The order of the turn: Alfen first and alone, then its sector, then its industry; and now its sector is turning up first while Alfen keeps falling. Alfen's Stock RS fell from 71 on 14-Aug to 30 on 31-Aug while its Sector RS held 73 to 87; the sector broke from 2-Oct and the industry from 9-Oct. Since 4-Nov the Sector RS has risen 13 points and Alfen's Stock RS has fallen 6.

Precedent: at Alfen's two earlier Stock RS lows its sector was strong and carried the recovery; this time the sector is near its own low. On 31-Oct-2025 its Stock RS was 3 with Sector RS 86, and the SP rose 19.3% from its 3-Nov-2025 low to 4-Feb-2026; on 20-Mar-2026, 12 with Sector RS 74, and the SP rose 138% to 26-May. Hypothesis: a sector recovery is necessary for an Alfen recovery but not enough, because Alfen broke before its sector; 9 and 10-Nov, a rising Sector RS with a falling Stock RS, fit it. Test: Sector RS above 50 on a Friday reading, with Alfen's Stock RS rising with it.

4. Consensus by named FY

Alfen's EBITDA consensus is falling for every year and its revenue consensus is edging up: more revenue at a lower margin, the pattern since the half-year results of 18-Aug. Nothing changed on 9 or 10-Nov; the last moves were on 4 to 6-Nov, after the results. (Years to December; EUR.)

  • EBITDA: FY2026 €21.65m, (0.4%) over 1 month, (14.4%) over 3 and (12.3%) over 6; FY2027 €27.95m, (2.9%), (9.0%) and (3.2%); FY2028 €33.71m, (2.2%), (6.5%) and (2.2%). Since 3-Nov, the eve of the results, FY2026 is down 2.3% and FY2027 2.6%.
  • Revenue: FY2026 €458.1m, FY2027 €495.5m, FY2028 €537.2m, each up 0.6% over 1 month and 1.3% to 1.8% over 3.
  • EPS: FY2026 a loss of €(0.140), against a €0.18 profit three months ago; FY2027 €0.350, (1.3%) over 1 month, +11.8% over 3; FY2028 €0.460, (7.8%) and (4.6%). On six or seven analysts and a near-zero base, EBITDA is the cleaner line.

The later years are cut as well as the nearer one, so analysts are lowering the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EBITDA margins are 4.7% (FY2026), 5.6% (FY2027) and 6.3% (FY2028). Implied EBITDA growth of 29.1% in FY2027 and 20.6% in FY2028, on revenue growth of 8.2% and 8.4%, places the whole recovery in the margin, and each round of cuts moves that recovery further out. Jefferies' model (the earnings review filed 10-Nov-2026) has the FY2027 margin at 5.7%, close to consensus, and a margin of 9% only by FY2030.

The narrowed guidance of 4-Nov points to a weak fourth quarter. On the APM's arithmetic, the narrowed ranges give €18.2m to €26.1m of FY2026 adjusted EBITDA; less the €20.4m delivered in nine months, the fourth quarter lies between €(2.2)m and €5.7m, a 1.6% margin at the midpoints, matching Deutsche Bank's "below 2%" (4-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026) and Jefferies' "breakeven" (the earnings review filed 10-Nov-2026).

Against the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): its FY2026 EBITDA forecast of €18.3m sits at the floor of the narrowed guidance; consensus (€21.65m) sits near the middle. The memo forecast "three downgrade steps", the first an FY2026 EBITDA cut "of 10% or more by February"; so far the cut since 3-Nov is 2.3%.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Alfen fails it on EBITDA, the line its analysts and its guidance use: FY2027 EBITDA is down 9.0% over 3 months and 3.2% over 6, FY2028 down 6.5% and 2.2%. Only revenue and the noisy FY2027 EPS line are rising.

The SP has fallen more than twice as fast as the estimates over three months ((21.2%) against (9.0%) for FY2027 EBITDA). Hypothesis: it is pricing the memo's further downgrade steps, as your note of 3-Nov puts it, "Almost always, price moves first". Test: whether €9.9 holds through the next round of cuts.

What the rules say for Alfen now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most for your decision on Alfen:

  • Alfen is weak against its own group, not only with it: on 10-Nov it failed a gap-up open and closed at the low ((2.1%) against the median peer's +0.2%), its relative price made its lowest close since 20-Mar, and its Stock RS fell to 12 while its Sector RS rose from 6 to 17 (items 1, 2 and 3).

4. Consensus by named FY

On 10-Nov, the day after the update, Theon's EPS consensus rose for every year (FY2026 +1.0%, FY2027 +3.0%, FY2028 +3.0%), but the number of analysts counted also rose from 7 to 9, so most of the rise may be two analysts returning to the sample rather than upgrades. Revenue moved little (FY2027 +0.2% to €754.5m), and FY2026 EBITDA fell 0.6% to €161.4m. Deutsche Bank, the one broker recorded after the update, made "no major changes" to revenue (earnings review), which fits a change of sample better than a round of upgrades.

The trend over the dashboard's periods: revenue and EBITDA rise for every year; EPS rises over every period only for FY2026.

  • FY2027: EPS down 1.0% over 1 and 3 months, flat over 6 and down 2.6% over 12; revenue up 1.9%, 2.5%, 6.5% and 13.2%; EBITDA up 3.6%, 5.7%, 10.5% and 18.3%.
  • FY2028: EPS down 0.7% over 3 months and up 13.4% over 12; revenue up 2.8% and EBITDA 4.7% over 3 months.
  • FY2026: EPS up 4.7% over 1 month and 11.4% over 3; revenue €601.3m, on guidance of about €600m.

The trajectory: EPS is pulled into FY2026 and pushed out to FY2028, while the operating lines grow evenly. Implied growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 4.3% for EPS against 25.5% for revenue and 28.7% for EBITDA; from FY2027 to FY2028, 19.6% for EPS against 16.9% and 17.8%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 26.8% (FY2026) to 27.5% (FY2027) and 27.7% (FY2028). The gap opens below EBITDA: Deutsche Bank's 5% EPS dilution from interest on about €573m of net debt after HGH (10-Nov).

The upgrade scope the update points to is in revenue, organic growth and EBIT, not yet in EPS. FY2027 revenue consensus of €754.5m is 25.5% above FY2026; taking out the €55m to €60m that Kappa, MERIO and HGH bring in 2027 (Deutsche Bank, 20-Jul-2026, in the earnings review), it is about 16% organic, against the new target of more than 20% a year. Deutsche Bank (10-Nov) puts consensus revenue about 5% and EBIT 7% to 9% below the company's targets. Test: FY2027 revenue and EBIT consensus over the four weeks after the update, with a stable analyst count.

Against your rule: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule" (3-Nov note). Theon still fails it on FY2027 EPS ((1.0%) over 3 months, flat over 6) and passes it on revenue and EBITDA. On 28-Oct you wrote: "Exit unless we can prove strong upgrade scope". The update gives scope on revenue and EBIT, on Deutsche Bank's reading; interest on the HGH debt keeps it from EPS, which the memo expects to fall in most months until the leverage question closes.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

On the update day the PT side weakened while the estimate side strengthened: the average PT fell 2.1% to €39.31, a third analyst cutting a PT appeared, and the first non-Buy rating since the September month-end was counted. Ratings read 8 Buys, 1 other, no Sells on 10-Nov, from 7, 0 and 0; Deutsche Bank kept Buy and €39, and no other broker is recorded after the update, so who cut and who rates Theon other than Buy are not in the sources read.

The ratings have followed valuation, not momentum (Buys rose from 5 to 7 at the November 2025 month-end as the SP fell 17.9%); a non-Buy two sessions after a lower low runs against that pattern. Test: whether it is a new Hold or a returning analyst.

Theon's average PT is 37.4% above the 10-Nov close, from 43.0% on 9-Nov. After gaps of about 35% at the November 2025 and June 2026 month-ends the SP rose about a quarter; after September 2026 (37.3%) it rose at most 6.5% before the 6-Nov lower low (previous note). The latest precedent is the one that failed, and the gap is wide because the SP fell, not because PTs rose (€40.50 at the June month-end, €39.31 now).

Net Upgrade Breadth: positive and rising on the operating lines, negative on PT, back to zero on EPS (10-Nov, with 9 or 10 analysts counted, from 7 or 8 on 9-Nov).

  • Rising: FY2027 EBITDA +78% (7 raising, none cutting, of 9), from +50% at the October month-end; FY2027 revenue +44% (4 of 9), from +25%.

Where analysts are cutting, and their stated logic: the balance sheet and the multiple, not the order book.

  • PTs: ING (15-Sep) cut to €38 from €42.50 on 16.1 times 2027 enterprise value to EBIT, "citing investor sensitivity to leverage", benchmarked to land-defence peers whose multiple fell from 21.6 to 17.2 times, while raising its forecasts (Sector Digest, Researcher's report of 29-Oct). The second and third PT cutters are not named in the sources read.
  • EPS: Deutsche Bank cut EPS 5% for interest after the 7-Sep report and repeats the 5% dilution now; the memo names UBS as carrying interest and amortisation in full (FY2027 EPS €1.42, the lowest).

This breadth carries the sell test SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50, a sell signal at the 30-Oct month-end (SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (25%), Stock RS 32). Would end it at 30-Nov: SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth back to zero, which now needs two PT raises with no new cut, or Stock RS of 50, 35 points away.

9. Peers

Theon's update did not separate it from its peers: from the 6-Nov low to 10-Nov Theon rose 7.0%, behind Hensoldt (+8.3%) and level with Ovzon (+7.2%), ahead of MilDef (+6.3%), Kongsberg Gruppen (+5.8%), Rheinmetall (+5.0%) and Exosens (+4.2%) (chart files). Your four peer checks, for the week to 10-Nov:

  • Newsflow: Rheinmetall's guidance cut and Boxer delay (5-Nov; item 1 (SP and MAs)); Kongsberg Gruppen and Thales filed for joint control of Thales Norway (European Union Eur-Lex, 3-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). Exosens has the cohort's strongest revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (+92%) yet trades with Theon: the market is pricing these two on something other than estimates.
  • SP technicals: Stock RS 98 at MilDef, 75 at Exail Technologies and 72 at Kongsberg Gruppen, all above their 50D and absolute 200D MAs; Hensoldt (33) is 4.1% above its 50D and 5.4% above its absolute 200D; Exosens (24) is 6.3% and 10.5% below them, beside Theon (15), 3.7% and 9.0% below.

Exosens is Theon's closest match on daily moves (average correlation 0.57, strong), and the link is in the business: Theon holds 9.8% of Exosens and is about 40% of its revenue (Bernstein, in the earnings review). The update widened the gap between them: Theon's Harder Digital stake, raised to 65.22%, lets it source tubes internally and "reduces reliance on external suppliers like Exosens" (Deutsche Bank, 9-Nov), and Exosens rose only 0.4% on 10-Nov on 0.96x. Hypothesis: the market treats the pair as one leveraged position on the soldier-modernisation trade. Test: a day when Theon rises while Exosens falls on volume.

Who turned first: down, Hensoldt and Exosens from 30-Oct, then Rheinmetall on 5-Nov; up, Hensoldt again, which regained its 50D MA while Theon is 3.7% below its own.

What the peers imply: Theon's direction into the Friday 13-Nov reading rests on Defence, through Hensoldt and Kongsberg Gruppen; the Bundestag budget committee (12-Nov) and vote (27-Nov) are the industry's next tests (memo, 5-Nov; Industry Digest). A Defence rally that lifts Industry RS to 30 would help Theon only through Defence, as in August; a further Rheinmetall-style cut would hit Theon at about twice Defence's move, as on 5-Nov.

On that test, with revenue and FY2026 EBITDA consensus cut over three and six months and EPS flat, Bally's Intralot fails.

  • Revenue (August month-end, the latest stored): FY2026 €1,128m, (1.9%) over 3 months and (1.3%) over 6; FY2027 €1,206m, (3.1%) and (2.6%); FY2028 €1,236m, (4.2%) and (5.2%). The later the year, the deeper the cut: analysts are lowering the trajectory, not only the year.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 €408.5m on 10-Nov, flat over 1 month and (2.7%) over 3; FY2027 €452.2m and FY2028 €473.4m (August month-end), (0.3%) and +0.8% over 3 months. The implied FY2026 EBITDA margin is about 36% on these figures.
  • EPS: one analyst; €0.02, €0.03 and €0.04 for FY2026 to FY2028 since 22-Sep, against €0.072, €0.086 and €0.097 at the July month-end: a change of contributor and basis, not a revision, which the dashboard nonetheless reads as an EPS revision of (65%) (a data fault).
  • The memo against consensus: it forecasts FY2026 EBITDA of €385m and FY2027 €400m, 5.8% and 11.5% below consensus, because the about €422m guidance sits above the chief executive's own divisional outlook (€380m to €400m).
  • SP against estimates: neither leads; both have been flat since September. Test: 27-Nov (projected), where the memo's markers for an upside surprise are United Kingdom growth above 12% and a quarter above €97m of EBITDA.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Bally's Intralot's sell-side readings rest on one or two analysts, nobody has moved since 22-Sep, and the risk is a single PT cut around 27-Nov.

  • Ratings: 2 Buys and nothing else on 10-Nov, as at the September and October month-ends. Over 24 months every change in the count came with a change in coverage; no rating followed the 23.0% fall to March or the 44.0% rise to June.
  • Average PT: €1.405, unchanged since 22-Sep, 25.7% above the €1.118 close. At the 23-Jun closing high the gap was 15%; a gap above 25% has not drawn buyers in four months.
  • Net Upgrade Breadth: PT 0% (2 analysts); revenue and EBITDA no reading since September; EPS 0% (1 analyst). The only non-zero readings of the year came from December 2025 to March 2026, at the SP's low.
  • Who is cutting: no broker in the sources read; the 6-Nov AlphaSense report found no new sell-side notes, and this week's was not read.
  • The link to "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50": with Stock RS at 37, one PT cut by either analyst before 30-Nov gives PT breadth of (50%) and a sell signal at the 30-Nov reading, unless Stock RS recovers above 50.

Allwyn's third quarter (19-Nov) and the Lottomatica and Cirsa votes in late November are the next peer readings before its own results.

What the rules say for Bally's Intralot now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most

The order of the turn: CVS Group first (Stock RS 25 to 32 in the week to 16-Oct while its Sector RS fell to 19), then Healthcare and the sector together in the week to 23-Oct; now CVS Group is the first to slip. The Sector RS fell on 10-Nov although its Nordic leaders made 52W closing highs (Attendo +3.3%, Ambea +1.2%); hypothesis: its weaker members are pulling it down (Emeis (2.8%), Clariane (2.0%) that day). Test: the Fri 13-Nov reading.

The precedents are warnings, with one difference. CVS Group's two earlier spells of Stock RS at 60 or more (10-Oct to 7-Nov-2025, from the first reading on file; 19-Dec-2025 to 20-Feb-2026, broken by 56, 51 and 59 on 23-Jan, 30-Jan and 6-Feb) ended in falls of 20.6% and 21.3%, with Healthcare's Industry RS at 26 to 59. What would confirm the strength: Stock RS back above 62 with Sector RS above 60 on Fri 13-Nov. What would refute it: Stock RS below 50, as it fell 17 points in the week to 25-Sep on a 6.2% results-day fall.

4. Consensus by named FY

CVS Group's FY2027 is its year to June 2027; FactSet consensus in pence and £m, latest point 10-Nov-2026, unchanged since the previous run.

CVS Group's SP has risen 16.7% in a month on estimates that rose about 1%: the price is leading, and the estimates have not followed.

  • EPS: FY2027 95.6p, +1.1% over 1 month, 0.0% over 3 and 12; FY2028 104.1p, +0.9% over 1 month, (0.7%) over 3, (1.4%) over 12; FY2029 113.1p, +1.6% over 1 month, (3.4%) over 3.
  • Revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 £751.0m and £150.8m (+0.4% and +0.3% over 1 month); FY2028 £785.3m and £159.1m (+0.6% each); FY2029 £823.8m and £167.5m (+2.1% and +1.1%, but EBITDA (3.7%) over 6 months).

The trajectory has not been raised: FY2028 and FY2029 EPS are lower over three and six months. Consensus implies EPS growth of 8.8% and 8.6% a year to FY2029 against EBITDA growth of 5.5% and 5.3%, the gap fitting the buyback; the implied EBITDA margin, 20.1% to 20.3%, sits near the bottom of the 19% to 23% target.

The broker detail (FactSet, refreshed 10-Nov) shows the Hold-rated brokers expecting the least growth. FY2027 EPS runs from Stifel's 93.1p (Hold, 2-Nov) to RBC Capital Markets' 100.8p (Buy, 2-Nov); Shore Capital (Hold, 8-Oct) has FY2028 EPS flat on FY2027 at 95.4p. The FY2028 spread, 95.4p to 112.1p, leaves room for the 25-Nov trading update to move consensus either way.

Against your rule: your 3-Nov market note says "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". CVS Group still fails it: FY2027 EPS is flat over 3 months, and FY2028 and FY2029 EPS are lower over 3 and 6 months.

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): it expects the late-November, January and 4-Mar-2027 statements to show group like-for-like growth of about 1.5% against 2.7% a year earlier, and FY2027 EPS consensus to drift to "about 92p to 94p by March", 2% to 4% below today's 95.6p. Implication: the memo expects the 25-Nov trading update to read as deceleration after a 21.1% rise from the 2-Oct low.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

The sell-side is leaning against CVS Group's SP: the average PT has fallen while the SP rose, a Buy has gone, and two of the three Hold-rated brokers have PTs below the price.

  • The average PT was 1,640p on 8-Oct, 1,626p on 14 and 23-Oct, 1,645p on 2-Nov and 1,605p since 3-Nov, a fall of 2.5% that day; the PT has been cut 1% over 1 month and 4% over 3 months on the dashboard's count.
  • Ratings went from 9 Buys and 2 others of 11 at the October month-end to 8 Buys and 3 others of 11 now; no analyst rates CVS Group a Sell. FactSet's nine named brokers split 6 Buy or Overweight to 3 Hold.
  • Ratings follow valuation more than momentum: Buys rose after falls (March 2025, July 2026) and slipped at highs (February 2026), as now.

The nine named PTs (FactSet, refreshed 10-Nov) run from 1,250p to 1,800p. RBC Capital Markets 1,800p (Buy, 2-Nov); Deutsche Bank 1,700p (Buy, 1-Oct); Peel Hunt 1,600p (Buy, 4-Nov); Investec 1,600p (Buy, 8-Oct); Davy 1,600p (Buy, 7-Oct); Barclays 1,600p (Overweight, 28-Sep); Jefferies 1,400p (Hold, 29-Sep), 1.4% above the 10-Nov close; Stifel 1,300p (Hold, 2-Nov), 5.8% below it; Shore Capital 1,250p (Hold, 8-Oct), 9.4% below it.

The gap between CVS Group's average PT and its SP is 16.3%, the narrowest since the August 2023 month-end, when the average PT of 2,428p stood 14.6% above the SP of 2,119p. It was 22.5% at 30-Oct and about 44% at the 2-Oct low. Precedent: after the August 2023 gap CVS Group fell 54.0% by the March 2024 month-end; after the May 2025 gap (21.0%) it rose on the Competition and Markets Authority's provisional decision. News, not the gap, set the direction.

Net Upgrade Breadth splits: analysts raise revenue and cut PTs and EPS.

  • PT: (27%), none raising and 3 cutting of 11, unchanged since 3-Nov; (18%) at 30-Oct, 0% at 30-Sep and +20% in July and August; the lowest in the 14 readings since October 2025.

The stated logic on file is Jefferies' (Hold, 1,400p; earnings review, 24-Sep): investors will await the new chief executive, and Healthy Pet Club membership falling to 508,000 "highlights structural volume pressure" that would force "downward revisions to FY27 forecasts" without a United Kingdom volume recovery. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov) reaches the same place: "the sell-side has bought the re-rating (73 percent Buy, median target 1,600p) without the estimates to support it, so the near-term breadth of revisions tips negative."

Healthy retest or broken trend? IMCD against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes, +3.0% over 20 sessions
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes, €94.09 against €90.70
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: passes, €99.09
  • The SP above each MA: passes, 0.6% above the 20D MA to 13.6% above the absolute 200D MA
  • Near its 52W high and away from its low: passes, 1.6% below the high, 49.6% above the low
  • Stage 3 red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, especially after good news: absent; the largest since 30-Jun are (4.4%) on 17-Jul and (4.2%) in the week to 15-Sep
  • Wider swings: absent; the average daily range over 1 month is 2.1% of the SP, against 2.2% over 3 months
  • Lower lows: absent; the intraday lows are rising (€98.04 on 22-Oct, €99.54 on 27-Oct, €100.45 on 5-Nov, €100.70 on 9-Nov, €101.80 on 10-Nov)
  • More down days than up days: partly; 10 down, 8 up and 1 flat since 14-Oct, but 14 of the last 20 closes sat in the top half of the day's range
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly; the 13 and 14-Oct breakout above the 4-May closing high (€101.14) came on 1.09x and 1.22x the 50D average volume and four distribution days followed; the SP is 1.9% above €101.14, so the breakout is not reversed
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; IMCD traded below its 20D MA intraday on 10 of the 14 sessions since 22-Oct and closed below it once

Verdict: a healthy retest more than a broken trend, narrowly. Every MA-structure test passes, the lows are rising and the closes sit high in the day's range; against that, heavy-volume selling followed a light-volume breakout, and the SP still hugs a 20D MA that has stopped rising. What settles it: a close above €104.7 on 1.5x the 50D average volume (about 200,000 shares) confirms the retest; a close below the 26-Oct closing low of €100.15 on heavy volume would make the breakout's reversal the working case.

Basis: one mechanical-volume day (18-Sep, an index review and option expiry) sits inside IMCD's 50D average volume and lowers each ratio by about 3%. The ratios below leave it out; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure follows in brackets.

On 10-Nov the volume dropped back to 0.94x (0.92x) on an up day: the supply eased, but no buyer stepped in on size.

  • 5, 6 and 9-Nov, three distribution days: (1.1%), (0.6%) and (0.5%) on 217,490, 203,067 and 193,100 shares (€22.5m, €20.9m, €19.7m), 1.70x, 1.57x and 1.48x (1.65x, 1.52x, 1.44x), closing at 61%, 33% and 82% of the day's range; 5-Nov opened at the €105.2 high, 6-Nov sold from €104.75, 9-Nov gapped 0.7% lower
  • 10-Nov: +0.8% on 123,378 shares (€12.7m), 0.94x (0.92x); no gap; fell to €101.80, below the 20D MA, then closed at 71% of the range
  • By your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average volume or more, over 25 sessions) IMCD has four distribution days (15-Oct, 5, 6 and 9-Nov) against two accumulation days (23-Oct, 4-Nov). The PMS Per-stock Dashboard's count was 1 on 3-Nov and is 4 today; 15-Oct leaves the 25-session window on about 18-Nov

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "L3D were Distribution Days - why. Azelis + Brenntag similar price movement" and "Only IMCD in its cohort had the higher volume for the price declines; Azelis didn't". Both are right, and the volume is IMCD's own; the price falls were not. From the 4-Nov close to 9-Nov IMCD fell 2.2% on 1.48x to 1.70x its 50D average volume; Brenntag fell 3.8% on 1.01x to 1.13x, and Azelis 1.8% on 0.43x to 0.79x. Why, hypothesis 1 (ranked first): holders used the results liquidity to sell between €101 and €105. For it: three heavy sessions with no close above €103.35, and the 6-Nov rally sold from €104.75. Against it: IMCD fell less than Brenntag on about one and a half times its relative volume, and closed at 61% and 82% of the range on two of the three days, which is what absorption looks like. 10-Nov's light up day fits both readings; the sources read do not say who sold.

On 10-Nov you also wrote: "But the SP was down 3D in a row post earnings, on higher than normal volume. Very negative signal IMHO". That is a negative signal and it is IMCD's own; the fall was small ((2.2%) in three sessions), and the fourth session closed back above the 20D MA.

Up-day against down-day volume has turned against IMCD since its high: the ratio of average up-day to average down-day volume is 0.81 (0.81) over 20 sessions and 0.60 on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard over 5 sessions, against 1.38 on 18-Aug; it is 0.69 over the 19 sessions since 14-Oct. Volume is rising into the pullback (20D against 50D average 1.14).

Volume at the MAs: IMCD's intraday breaks of the 20D MA rose from 0.84x to 1.35x the 50D average volume (22 to 29-Oct) to 1.70x and 1.57x (5 and 6-Nov); the close below came on 1.48x and the close back above on 0.94x. For any new test of tranche 1 the volume reading decides: from day 2, a close still at or below the 20D MA, with the sessions after day 1 averaging 0.75x the 50D average volume (about 100,000 shares) or more, confirms the exit; a close back above the MA ends the test instead. IMCD traded less than 0.75x on only three of the last 25 sessions (12, 19 and 21-Oct), so two consecutive closes at or below the 20D MA would very likely confirm the exit.

Precedent: IMCD's declines in the last 12 months ended with 5% to 6.3% up days on 1.2x to 1.7x the 50D average volume (21-Jan, 23-Mar, 14-Jul), not with selling climaxes; 10-Nov's +0.8% on 0.94x is not that sign.

Relative price has not confirmed the SP's 14-Oct high: 7.3% below its 4-May 52W high (7.7% on 9-Nov, 4.6% on 3-Nov, 2.6% at the 14-Oct SP high), and 6.8% above its rising relative 200D MA. Relative price ticked up on 10-Nov (55.86 from 55.61), its second rise since the 3-Nov level of 57.48, after 6-Nov's (56.35 to 56.44).

What would confirm a turn up: a close above €104.7 on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more (about 200,000 shares), with relative price back above its 3-Nov level. What would confirm the move continues down: a close at or below the 20D MA followed by a second close at or below it on 100,000 shares or more (tranche 1's exit confirmed), then a close below €100.15 on heavy volume.

The trend is intact over every period. Carlsberg is up 2.4% over the week from the 3-Nov close (1.7 points of it the Stoxx Europe 600's, at a beta of 0.87), 14.5% over 1 month, 11.9% over 3 and 37.1% over 12. Over 20 sessions Carlsberg's own part is 11.3 points, 1.7 times its usual 20-session move: the October advance from DKK 840.6 on 18-Sep (+21.9%) is the large move, and this week is quiet inside it.

The legs since the 22-Oct high, against the brewers and Consumer staples:

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Carlsberg's three sessions off its 5-Nov high have come on falling volume with strong closes: the volume of a pause, not of distribution. On 10-Nov Carlsberg rose 0.7% on 180,558 shares, DKK 185.1m, 0.78x its 50D average volume (0.79x without the one mechanical-volume day in 50 sessions, 18-Sep, which changes no reading here), with no gap, closing at 0.95 of the day's range after a low of DKK 1,007.0. On 9-Nov it fell 0.8% on 0.84x, closing at 0.50 of its range; on 6-Nov it fell 0.5% on 1.33x after a 2.1% gap down but closed at the day's high. All five closes since 4-Nov sat at or above mid-range, and 17 of the last 20.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) in the last 25 sessions: three, none since 30-Oct. 15-Oct, +3.6% on 1.48x (1.54x without mechanical-volume days), DKK 299.9m, closing at 0.89 of the range, a brewers' day; 27-Oct, (1.4%) on 2.07x (2.14x), DKK 445.1m, closing at 0.11, Carlsberg's own before the statement; 30-Oct, +3.9% on 2.43x (2.51x), DKK 551.1m, a 2.2% gap up, the post-statement reversal.

Distribution against accumulation, 25 sessions: 3 distribution days (27 and 29-Oct around the statement, and 6-Nov, whose close at the day's high reads as demand absorbing the gap down) against 6 accumulation days (7, 14, 15, 28 and 30-Oct, and 2-Nov). The dashboard's count rose from 1 at 3-Nov to 3, all tied to the statement week or the 6-Nov gap.

Up-day against down-day volume: 1.02 over 20 sessions and 0.85 over 5 (the dashboard's figures). Over the advance from 18-Sep (+21.9%, 37 sessions) the ratio is 1.03, against 1.26 and 1.16 for the advances to February and May 2026 and 1.02 for June to July 2026, which gave way to the 12.9% fall. Thin up-day volume is still the advance's weak point: the 20D average volume runs at 1.26x the 50D average, but buyers and sellers are evenly matched.

The trend of volume through the move: light from 16 to 26-Oct (0.63x to 1.14x) while the SP rose 3.1%, heavy around the statement (27-Oct to 2-Nov), lighter since: a breakout resting on lighter volume, the textbook sequence.

Volume at the MAs: the 150D and absolute 200D MAs were regained on 7-Oct on 1.36x, the 50D and 100D on 12-Oct on only 0.81x, and the DKK 981.8 ceiling on 2-Nov on 1.32x. No MA has been tested since.

Precedent. Of Carlsberg's last three lows (24-Mar, 4-Jun, 18-Sep) only March's turned on heavy-volume up days within ten sessions: its turns come quietly. How its tops began matters more: after the 12-Feb high Carlsberg fell 1.8% on 1.67x the next day, closing at 0.26 of its range, before a 20.0% fall; after the 30-Jul high volume dried up and the 12.9% fall began with a 20D MA break on 10-Aug on 1.06x. Neither pattern is present: no heavy down day closing near its low, and the 20D MA is 4.8% below the close.

Carlsberg's relative price has not confirmed the SP's 5-Nov high and lost more ground this week. It is 5.7% below its 52W high of 12-Feb (4.6% on the 5-Nov SP high, 4.4% on 6-Nov, 6.1% on 9-Nov) and 8.7% above its relative 200D MA, which it crossed on 15-Oct. An SP high without a relative-price high says part of the advance is the market's: confirmation needs about 6.1% more outperformance of the Stoxx Europe 600.

Peers' volume on 10-Nov (my calculation from the chart files): AB InBev 1.03x and Heineken 1.21x, each closing at a 52W high; Royal Unibrew 1.14x. Carlsberg's 0.78x was the lightest of the brewers.

Confirms the advance: a pullback towards DKK 981.8 and the rising 20D MA on volume below the 50D average that holds, then an up day on 1.5x or more closing in its top third, or a close above DKK 1,031.5 on above-average volume. Confirms a failure: a close below DKK 981.8 on above-average volume, then below the 29-Oct low of DKK 934.2 (8.9% below the close).

Newsflow and guidance (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026 and the sources named; nothing newer was read):

What the peers imply for Carlsberg. Beverages' Sector RS (81), one of the two halves of Carlsberg's nearest sell test (Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70), is rising on spirits and the large brewers, so a sell signal needs a group-wide fall, as from the 31-Jul to the 31-Aug month-end. The signal to watch is relative, not absolute: Carlsberg underperforming AB InBev and Heineken by more than its usual weekly move of 3.3% while they hold their highs would point to the China gap, not the rotation.

What the rules say for Carlsberg now, and what decides the next step

  • Most important: Carlsberg is pausing while its peers make new highs. On 10-Nov AB InBev and Heineken closed at 52W highs and Carlsberg 0.6% below its 5-Nov high, its relative price 5.7% below its own and unconfirmed.   • Second: the sell-side is broadening behind the SP, but the size is small and one Buy has gone. 2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: every multiple of the 50D average volume leaves out the 18-Sep index-review and option-expiry day; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure is in brackets.

Hilton Food made its new closing high on Tuesday on its lightest volume in more than ten months: the high was reached without buyers pressing.

  • 10-Nov: +1.3% to 748.5p on 62,981 shares (£0.47m), 0.33x (0.32x) the 50D average, its lowest volume since 31-Dec-2025 (50,296 shares). The SP opened 0.7% lower at 734.0p, fell to 730.0p, reached 752.5p and closed at 0.82 of the day's range; LDC (1.57x) and Bell Food Group (1.64x) traded heavily that day, so the light volume was Hilton Food's own. On 13 to 19-Oct, when Hilton Food's own volume was heavy, only Greencore (2.7x and 2.1x) and Cranswick (1.8x on 15-Oct) had matched it among the peers.
  • The week: the breakout day, 4-Nov (+2.8% on 255,463 shares, £1.90m, 1.31x (1.28x), closing at the day's high), is still the only session above 1.0x since 20-Oct. 6-Nov opened at 755.0p, the intraday high of 5-Nov, and closed (1.2%) lower at 735.0p on 0.43x (0.42x) the 50D average: a gap-up rejected at the same level two days running, but on light volume.

The heavy volume of the last 25 sessions all sits inside the base, on 13 to 19-Oct; nothing since has reached 1.5x the 50D average. 13-Oct (+1.5% on 602,397 shares, £4.30m, 3.20x (3.14x)), 14-Oct (+0.1%, 2.27x (2.23x)) and 19-Oct (+0.4%, 2.42x (2.38x)) were accumulation days; 15-Oct ((0.7%) on 308,992 shares, £2.19m, 1.63x (1.60x), closing at 0.15 of the range) is the only distribution day. Accumulation days outnumber distribution days 4 to 1 in 25 sessions (4-Nov the fourth); the distribution count was 1 at 13-Oct, 2 at 27-Oct and 3-Nov, and 1 now. 15-Oct leaves the 25-session window on 19-Nov, and the distribution count then falls to 0 unless a new distribution day comes.

Up-day volume still leads down-day volume, but the ratio fell this week as the October buying left the window, not because sellers came in. Hilton Food's average up-day volume divided by its average down-day volume is 1.35 over 20 sessions (1.91 on 6-Nov, 1.60 on 9-Nov) and 1.36 over 5 sessions (2.12 on 9-Nov), the dashboard's figures. The 20-session ratio fell as 13-Oct's 602,397 shares left the window on 9-Nov and 14-Oct's 425,369 shares left it on 10-Nov. The 5-session ratio fell on 10-Nov because the 4-Nov breakout day's 255,463 shares left the window and Tuesday's up day added only 62,981 shares. Over the whole advance from the 29-Jan low the average up day traded 1.12 times the average down day, against 0.79 in the 2025 decline from 905.1p.

Volume has dried up through the base and the breakout: the last 10 sessions averaged 120,283 shares, 0.62x the 50D average (the dashboard's volume trend). Hypothesis, ranked first: sellers are absent, not buyers present. For it: the falls of 3 and 6-Nov came on 0.66x and 0.43x, and no down day since 15-Oct has reached 1.0x. Against it: Hilton Food's new high came on 0.33x, and Richard's Stage 3 (topping) checklist of 10-Nov names "Breakouts upwards are on lighter volume than down days" as a test. On the 5-session ratio (1.36), the average up day still carries more volume than the average down day (113,780 shares against 6-Nov's 83,821), so that test does not yet read yes.

A mechanical change this week will make the same trading look heavier. The heavy sessions of 2 to 4-Sep, the 3-Sep results day (1.03M shares) among them, leave the 50D window on 11, 12 and 13-Nov. If daily volume stays at its 10-session average, Hilton Food's 50D average volume (the dashboard's basis) falls from 194,970 shares to about 170,600 by Fri 13-Nov, 12% lower, so 1.5x becomes about 256,000 shares (about £1.9m at 748.5p). 4-Nov's 255,463 shares would then read about 1.5x.

Precedent: how Hilton Food's earlier bases ended. May, the failure: the 19-May breakout (+2.7% on 1.76x) reversed on 28-May ((5.2%) on 2.15x), and the SP fell 9.4% in five sessions to 1-Jun. August, the success: the base dried up to 0.42x on 26-Aug, then broke out on rising volume (27-Aug 1.00x, 28-Aug 1.58x) before the 3-Sep results added 12.2% on 5.92x. Today's base matches August's dry-up and has had no heavy reversal, but lacks August's rising volume on the breakout days.

Hilton Food's relative price has not confirmed the SP's new high: on 10-Nov it stood 0.6% below its 8-Oct 52W high, the same distance as on 5-Nov, and 21.6% above its relative 200D MA, which rose 3.28% over the month to 10-Nov. With a beta of 0.35, Hilton Food gains relative ground mainly on days the market falls; its relative price has made no new 52W high in the 23 sessions since 8-Oct, while the SP made six new closing highs (20 and 21-Oct, 2, 4, 5 and 10-Nov).

What would confirm the turn up: a close above 755.0p on volume above about 256,000 shares (1.5x the 50D average from 13-Nov), with no heavy-volume reversal after it. What would confirm a failure: a close below 734.0p, the base's top, on 1.5x the 50D average volume, or a distribution day that closes below the 20D MA (725.7p), the first close below it since 9-Jul.

Hilton Food's Stock RS reached 94 on Tuesday, its highest reading since the turn; Food production's Sector RS fell to 44 the same day, below its line of 50.

  • Hilton Food's Stock RS: 94 on 10-Nov, from 89 at the 6-Nov Friday reading, 87 at the 30-Oct month-end and 90 a month ago; 81 or more every Friday since 4-Sep, against lines of 70 and 50. After two flat months it is rising again.
  • Food production's Sector RS: 44 on 10-Nov, from 54 on 9-Nov, 57 at the 6-Nov reading and 64 on 4-Nov: 20 points in four sessions, and 6 below the line of 50. On 10-Nov Food production's members mostly rose (Bell Food Group +2.5%, Cranswick +1.1%, Danone +1.0%, LDC +0.9%) and Consumer staples rose 0.5% against the Stoxx Europe 600's 0.3%, so a 10-point fall that day is not explained by the day's moves; the cause is not in the data read (the reading's lookback dropping strong days is one possibility, unverified).

The order of the turn: Hilton Food first and alone, then the industry, with the sector lagging and now falling back. Hilton Food's Stock RS rose from 19 (24-Jul) to 84 (4-Sep) while Sector RS stayed between 29 and 43; Industry RS rose from 33 (2-Oct) to 73 (23-Oct). The Industry Digest's de-grossing hypothesis of 30-Oct ("staples falling on days Industrials rise supports de-grossing") has not been borne out since: on 9 and 10-Nov Industrials and capital goods rose 1.6% and 0.3% and Consumer staples rose 0.3% and 0.5%.

Precedent: Food production's Sector RS had three earlier spells above 50 this year: six Fridays (6-Mar to 10-Apr), five (24-Apr to 22-May) and two (10 and 17-Jul); the current spell is four Fridays (16-Oct to 6-Nov), and the 29-Oct dip to 48 recovered to 55 by the next day. Test, Fri 13-Nov: Sector RS at or above 50 keeps the spell and Hilton Food's place in the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection safe; a second Friday below 50 (13 and 20-Nov) would remove Hilton Food from the Pool (closing paragraph). Sector RS is the Hilton Food reading nearest any line.

4. Consensus by named FY

Hilton Food's EPS upgrades have stalled since 8-Oct, and the later FYs rise more slowly than the nearer one. (Financial year to December; sterling. No consensus figure moved on 9 or 10-Nov.)

  • EPS: FY2026 52.2p, +1.5% over 1 month, +6.9% over 3, +9.7% over 6; FY2027 56.9p, +1.0%, +6.1%, +8.1%; FY2028 62.1p, (0.7%), +2.9%, +3.9%. No FY has moved more than 0.1% since 8-Oct.
  • The shape: FY2026 has risen fastest and FY2028 slowest over every period, so analysts are lifting the near year (the 3-Sep guidance raise to £66m to £71m adjusted PBT, which Shore Capital called a technical adjustment, about £3.7m of it from treating Dalco as discontinued, per the earnings review), not the trajectory; implied EPS growth is 9.0% into FY2027 and 9.1% into FY2028.
  • Revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 revenue £4,798m (+1.3% over 1 month, +5.4% over 3), largely raw-material prices passed through at cost; FY2026 to FY2028 EBITDA £149.8m, £160.5m and £168.2m (+4.3%, +3.4% and +2.5% over 1 month), implied EBITDA margins 3.25%, 3.35% and 3.39%. The month's EBITDA rise (the same £4.2m in each FY on 4-Nov, with EPS unmoved) reads as one analyst changing basis, not an upgrade.
  • Deutsche Bank sits 2.5% below consensus on FY2027 revenue (£4,676m), with FY2026 and FY2027 PBT of £68m and £76m (Deutsche Bank Research, 5-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026).

Hilton Food's SP has run far ahead of its estimates: +37.9% over 6 months against FY2027 EPS +8.1%, so the multiple did most of the work; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (4-Nov) puts it at about 11.75x two-year-forward earnings, the five-year median, and judges the market "has paid for the repair. It has not paid for growth". Your 3-Nov market note's rule ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule") is met over 3 and 6 months; over 1 month the upgrades have stopped.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO the case needs another fundamental leg of delivery for SP to power onwards or it'll retrace hard". No new leg has arrived, yet the SP made a new high without one. The memo dates the next legs: the NADEC joint venture in Saudi Arabia (fourth quarter of 2026), the Polish capacity decision (end-2026) and Walmart Canada (January 2027); it also judges the company "an honest tracker, not an upgrader", so guidance-led upgrades in the next 12 months are unlikely.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings and PTs follow its SP: the analysts upgraded after the rise, not after the falls. Buys held at 4 or 5 through most of the 2025 decline and settled at 3 only after the 11-Nov-2025 profit warning, when the average PT fell 32% in a month (1,071p to 730p). Buys fell to 2 at the July and August 2026 month-ends as the SP rose and coverage fell from 6 to 4, then rose to 4 after the 3-Sep results. All 4 analysts with a rating say Buy (10-Nov).

The gap between the average PT and the SP is closing again: 6.2% now (795p against 748.5p), from 7.6% on 9-Nov and 11.2% on 8-Oct. It was 44.6% at the January 2026 month-end, 29.5% at June and 3.9% at the 30-Sep month-end, the narrowest since at least August 2024. The average has been 795p since 8-Oct; the latest named PT is Deutsche Bank's 850p, reiterated with Buy on 5-Nov on "core business stabilization", which Deutsche Bank puts at 21% upside from the 723p of 3-Nov, though 850p is 17.6% above 723p (Deutsche Bank Research, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). Implication: at the next leg up the SP meets the average PT unless analysts raise it, as they did at the 30-Sep month-end, when the 3.9% gap was followed by a 6.4% PT rise on 8-Oct.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure, but one analyst is cutting FY2027 EPS and no new raise has come since early October.

  • EPS (FY2027): +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5), from +60% (3 raising, none cutting) at the July and August month-ends. EBITDA +60% (3 raising, none cutting); revenue +40% (2 raising, none cutting, of 5); PT +50% (2 raising, none cutting, of 4). Unchanged since the 30-Oct month-end.
  • Where analysts are cutting: the sources read do not name the analyst cutting FY2027 EPS or give the logic. The memo names the visible brokers as Deutsche Bank, Peel Hunt and Shore Capital and puts Peel Hunt's 720p PT "at the price"; Peel Hunt is the likeliest candidate, but that is an inference, not a source.
  • The drift: the raises after the 3-Sep results leave the 100-day window over which Net Upgrade Breadth counts revisions, during December, so without new raises revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth will drift towards zero ahead of the 31-Dec month-end reading.

9. Peers

Hilton Food led its cohort out of the trough; over the past month the laggards caught up, but this week Hilton Food was the second-best of the five. (The table and chart are on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard.)

  • SP technicals: over the month to 10-Nov Greencore rose 15.1%, LDC 11.0%, Bell Food Group 9.4%, Cranswick 8.8% and Hilton Food 6.4%, the cohort's slowest; over the week Cranswick rose 4.1%, Hilton Food 3.5%, Bell Food Group 2.3%, Greencore 2.0% and LDC 1.7%. All five are above their 50D MAs.

4. Consensus by named FY

Fevertree's consensus has not moved since the previous run, and over 3 months no FY2026 to FY2028 line has risen: the SP's 9.1% rise over 3 months came with no estimate support.

  • EPS: FY2026 28.0p, (0.4%) over 1M, flat over 3M, (2.0%) over 6M; FY2027 38.3p, (0.5%), flat, +0.2%; FY2028 48.6p, (0.3%), (1.0%), +1.7%.
  • Revenue: FY2026 £400.6m, (0.1%) over 1M, (0.5%) over 3M; FY2027 £433.0m, (0.3%), (1.2%), and (3.1%) over 12M; FY2028 £464.6m, (0.4%), (2.6%), and (5.0%) over 12M.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 £49.8m, flat over 1M, (0.7%) over 3M; FY2027 £63.9m, (0.3%), (1.6%); FY2028 £77.6m, (0.4%), (4.0%), and (4.9%) over 12M.

The later years are cut fastest, so analysts are trimming the trajectory the case rests on, not the current year. FY2028 EBITDA fell 4.0% over 3 months against FY2026's 0.7%. Consensus still has EBITDA growing 28.3% in FY2027 and 21.5% in FY2028 on revenue growth of 8.1% and 7.3%, so the implied EBITDA margin climbs from 12.4% (FY2026) to 14.7% (FY2027) and 16.7% (FY2028). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (4-Nov) puts its own base 15% below consensus on FY2027 EPS and 20% below on FY2028, and judges a 5% to 9% cut to FY2027-28 EBITDA consensus the probable path, concentrated in United States revenue growth (consensus +18% in FY2027 against the memo's +12%). The SP has led and the estimates have not followed. Your 3-Nov market note expects estimates to follow price only in a sector low in its range ("Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"), and Beverages is not low in its range: Sector RS 81, and nine of the eleven Beverages stocks 10% or more above their absolute 200D MAs. So the note does not predict that Fevertree's estimates will catch up; its nearer line, "Sectors where price momentum is strong but EPS is not is potentially start of inflection", still needs upgrades to prove the inflection. The same note's "inviolate rule" on upgrades is in the closing paragraph.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I must get the Nielsen check and see what it says". The two scanner research files filed on 10-Nov add no United States print after 17-Oct, and both regions' latest four-week readings are slower than their twelve-week readings.

  • United States: the latest readings remain Barclays' NielsenIQ series, Fever-Tree up 15.4% in the four weeks to 3-Oct and 11.9% to 17-Oct (scanner tracker, 4-Nov). The new research adds that Labor Day fell on 7-Sep in 2026 against 1-Sep in 2025, which "significantly boosted the 4-week period ending October 3, 2026" (Morgan Stanley, via the United States scanner research filed 10-Nov). So the 15.4% overstates the run-rate, and 11.9% is the cleaner reading: short of the memo's own condition for changing its view (the scanner rate "holding above 15% into the holiday prints") and of the 20% your 28-Oct note sets.
  • Great Britain: Fevertree's retail sales rose 5.4% in value (4.9% in volume) in the four weeks to 4-Oct, against 10.4% for all soft drinks, after 12.9% over twelve weeks (Morgan Stanley, via the United Kingdom and Europe scanner research filed 10-Nov).
  • Europe: Fevertree's sales rose 6.6% in the four weeks to 4-Oct against 11.2% over twelve weeks (Morgan Stanley); Jefferies has Europe outside Great Britain slowing to 8.1% in October from 10.9% (same research).

Test: the next United States four-week print against 15% (the first without the Labor Day shift), and FY2027 EBITDA consensus after the January 2027 pre-close.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Fevertree's SP is pinned under a flat average PT (947.3p): six intraday pushes to 943p to 955p since 23-Oct, and no close above 943.5p; the close is 1.7% below it. The average PT has stood at 947.3p at every daily reading since 6-Oct (967.3p at the 31-Jul month-end, 948.9p at 31-Aug); the median PT, about 875p, is 6.0% below the close (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 3-Nov). The two latest times the SP reached the average PT (the July 2025 and February 2026 month-ends), Fevertree fell 14.9% and 20.0% by the next month-end (18.3% and 21.3% to the lowest close within 22 sessions). Test of the ceiling: a close above 947.3p with PTs rising.

Ratings, PT and Net Upgrade Breadth are unchanged since the 30-Oct month-end. Fevertree has 7 Buys, 5 other and 3 Sells (15 analysts), unchanged since 30-Sep; its Buys have followed falls (to 5 after the February-to-May fall, to 7 after the 10-Sep low), ratings that follow valuation, so new Buys are unlikely near the average PT. Net Upgrade Breadth for FY2027: EPS 0% (6 raising, 6 cutting, of 15), from +50% at the July month-end; EBITDA +36% (7 raising, 2 cutting, of 14), yet FY2027 EBITDA consensus fell 0.3% over the month, so the raises are small and the cuts larger; revenue (9%) (2 raising, 3 cutting, of 11), negative at every month-end since August 2025 except July 2026; PT (13%) (3 raising, 5 cutting, of 15), from (7%) at 30-Sep. The breadth diverges: EBITDA raisers outnumber cutters while revenue and PT cutters outnumber raisers, which fits analysts trimming the United States top line while keeping the margin step.

Where analysts are cutting: revenue, PTs and the later years. Sell-side Momentum is rated D (9-Nov). The brokers behind the five PT cuts and three revenue cuts in the latest 100-day counts are not named in the sources read, and no AlphaSense report was read this run. The stated logic on file points at the United States growth rate: Rothschild & Co Redburn (Sell, 700p) argues that consensus United States growth of 18% a year is far too optimistic while the United States spirits market declines (15-Apr-26); Deutsche Bank (Hold, 900p) cut its five-year United States revenue growth rate to 14% from 14.4%, saying the market needs to see compounding growth "closer to twenty percent" to justify higher valuation multiples (6-Oct-26); both via the United States sales bridge research (4-Nov), with the same Deutsche Bank note's "compounding near 20%" in the United States depletions research (15-Oct). What would name the cutters: a wider AlphaSense search for Fevertree PT notes since the 10-Sep results, which this unattended run could not make.

9. Peers

SP technicals: for a second week Fevertree trades with the small soft-drinks names, not with the alcohol makers whose daily moves usually track it. Over the five sessions to 10-Nov Fevertree rose 1.1%, with AG Barr +0.2% and Royal Unibrew +0.2%, while the four stocks whose daily moves track Fevertree most closely rose 2.4% to 5.7%: AB InBev +5.2%, Carlsberg +2.4% (a holding), Pernod Ricard +5.7% and Remy +4.8% (average correlation 0.37 to 0.39, moderate). Diageo rose 7.5%, Campari 7.8% and Coca-Cola Europacific Partners 5.4%. Hypothesis: inside Beverages the money favours the large companies and the spirits houses over the smaller soft-drinks companies, which fits your 28-Oct worry of "rotation into the "big boys" stocks like Diageo out of Fevertree". Evidence against: Fevertree is still up 7.2% over 20 sessions, and its tie to the alcohol makers is only moderate. Test: whether Fevertree rises with the alcohol makers on their next up days.

Sell-side estimates: Fevertree's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((9%)) is one of three negatives in Beverages, with AB InBev ((20%)) and Pernod Ricard ((5%)); Carlsberg (+60%), Remy (+53%) and Campari (+48%) lead. Upgrades do not rank the SP moves: Coca-Cola HBC has a +30% 3-month revenue revision and the weakest SP ((10.9%) over the week).

Newsflow and company guidance (via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026; nothing newer was read): Diageo's first-quarter trading statement beat consensus (Deutsche Bank, 5-Nov), and Coca-Cola HBC raised its 2026 organic revenue guidance to slightly above 7% (Morgan Stanley, 4-Nov), yet Coca-Cola HBC has fallen 13.9% since 4-Nov, a cause not in the sources read. Molson Coors, Fevertree's United States partner, filed its third-quarter statements on 5-Nov (cited in the United States scanner research filed 10-Nov, not in the AlphaSense report of 6-Nov); what Molson Coors said about Fevertree that week, an event the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo names as able to change its view, is not in the sources read. The same research records Morgan Stanley (November 2026) on Molson Coors' "structural pressures on alcohol consumption and declining brand volumes": the partner's beer network is shrinking. Fevertree's own guidance is unchanged since the 10-Sep results.

What the peers imply: the sector's strength no longer lifts Fevertree on the way up, while a sector reversal would still weigh on it: the 22 to 29-Oct fall in Consumer staples ((1.2%), while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8%) took Fevertree down 3.2%. A repeat of that 3.2% fall from the 931.0p close would take Fevertree to about 901p, below its 20D MA and still about 2.8% above the 50D MA as it would stand on 13-Nov at a flat SP (876.7p).

What the rules say for Fevertree now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • Fevertree is losing ground inside a rotation that is still running: +1.1% over the week against Consumer staples' +2.4%; Stock RS 57 while Beverages' Sector RS rose to 81 (items 1, 3 and 9).

  • Nothing fundamental pulls the other way: no estimate up over 3 months, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth negative, and the cleaner United States scanner reading is 11.9% (items 4, 5 and 6).

Tranche 1 (2.47% of capital now, 2.0% at entry; 50D MA) is clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule: the 931.0p close is 7.0% above the 50D MA (870.21p) and 13.8% above the stop-loss (818.00p). Its exit confirmed on 3-Sep-2026 was cleared under your 2-Nov-2026 ruling (the price had since closed back above the MA); it is judged afresh from Monday 2-Nov.

The nearest sell test is tranche 1's 50D MA, and the rising 50D is closing the gap.

  • Pace: a 6.5% fall from the 931.0p close to the 50D MA starts a 10-day stop-loss test; the close stood 6.8% above the 50D a week ago, 3.1% four weeks ago and 4.2% thirteen weeks ago. At a flat SP the 50D reaches 876.7p by Friday 13-Nov and 890.1p by Friday 20-Nov, cutting the fall needed to 5.8% and 4.4%.
  • What the rule then does: Fevertree's down days since 22-Oct ran 0.76x to 1.32x the 50D average, so a second close at or below the 50D on ordinary volume would very likely confirm the exit on day 2 (the days-after volume average reaching 0.75 times the 50-day average), selling tranche 1 at the following Tuesday session. A fall to the stop-loss would cost about 0.30% of capital.
  • How the tests interact: the same fall would very likely take Stock RS below 50, which with negative revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth would give a Core Investment a sell signal at the 30-Nov month-end; for Fevertree it is information only.
  • The record: the 50D test from 2-Sep confirmed on 3-Sep at 814.9p, before a further 5.7% fall; one from 1-Oct would have confirmed on 2-Oct at 833.5p, before a 13.2% rise.

The next reading day is Friday 13-Nov-2026: Consumer staples' Industry RS (73) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month ((0.31%) on 10-Nov, about (0.41%) on 13-Nov at a flat relative price; clearing it needs closes near 1,178p, 26.5% higher, on each of 11 to 13-Nov). Both are information only for Fevertree as a Probing Bet.

The rules allow an add (tranche 1 clear, 1 of 2 tranches, 3.53% of room below the 6% limit; the next tranche sizes at the 3% cap). Where Fevertree stands for an add: the second of the three stages, may be retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern, up from the first stage at the 9-Nov run: 10-Nov bounced off the rising 20D on 1.47x, and the dips from each push have shrunk, from each push's intraday high to the following low, from 6.2% (949.0p on 26-Oct to 890.5p on 29-Oct) to 5.1% (2 to 3-Nov), 4.9% (5 to 6-Nov) and 3.2% (9 to 10-Nov).   • For your ruling: your 3-Nov rule, "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule", bears on Fevertree, which has not upgraded over 3 or 6 months; the Position Entry and Exit Rules do not yet contain it.
